# Beyond Sampling — results ledger

This page explains what we have learned, how much confidence to place in it, and what remains to be done. Our goal is to predict a neural network’s mistakes by understanding its calculations, rather than relying only on large numbers of random tests.

[Project overview](README.md) · [Interactive Tutorial](https://claude.ai/artifact/22zmMZh9WU6LAPLWqJmXRX) · [Current work](TASKS.md)

## What we have achieved

**There are two candidates for the full ARC challenge. Neither has been accepted by ARC.** Their measured performance is encouraging, but we have not established a solution that meets the strictest requirement: deriving the estimate from the network without relying on unproved assumptions about unexamined inputs.

| Result | What it means | What remains uncertain |
|---|---|---|
| **Our method outperformed ARC’s published methods for the smaller challenge in our reproduction.** | ARC described its two methods for a four-neuron, three-number network as roughly matching random testing. Our mechanism-derived method achieved about **50 times lower squared error than sampling** under the tested computation comparison; an independent check confirmed our advantage over sampling. | The comparison concerns ARC’s published smaller-model examples, not its reported unpublished full-challenge solution. Our result is scoped to the studied trained network and specified weight changes, and is not a runtime speedup. |
| **One candidate beats random testing at the same compute budget.** | Following the network exactly along selected input slices, then combining them, gave about **half the squared error** of random testing on a fresh panel of networks trained for the same task. Independently scored. | We lack a proof that the selected slices represent the rest of the input space accurately enough. This result does not establish performance on the original target or copies with noisy weights. |
| **A second candidate uses no random testing while it runs and was tested at a much smaller budget.** | A fixed set of shortcuts about how the network makes mistakes gave about **2.6 times the squared error** of equal-compute random testing. This supports a possible “rough match” interpretation of the challenge. Independently scored. | Random testing is still more accurate at that budget. The shortcuts were developed using sampled data, and the method fails on noisy weights. ARC’s acceptance is unresolved. |
| **Some extremely rare mistakes can be estimated without billions of ordinary trials.** | On certain tested cases, methods using the network’s structure estimated failure probabilities around **one in billions**. Independent checks support the stated cases. | Other failure patterns are badly underestimated. We cannot assume these methods reliably find every rare failure. |
| **A small, targeted weight edit can reduce a rare mistake.** | Changing **12 weights** made one studied mistake **17–19 times less likely**. A separate implementation reproduced the result. | Another type of mistake became more common. Fixing one failure does not by itself establish an overall safety improvement. |
| **We have useful components for mathematical probability bounds.** | Reviewed calculations can bound the probability of certain input regions under explicit numerical assumptions. | The complete chain—from covering all relevant inputs to adding their probabilities—has not been verified for the full challenge. Component checks are not a whole-network guarantee. |
| **Independent checks caught two important reliability problems.** | We reproduced a numerical error in one proposed probability bound and showed that an importance-sampling warning can miss failure modes. A local numerical repair and a conservative reporting alternative were checked. | These repairs still need integration and validation on the full trained-network workflow. The findings do not invalidate every other bound or saved experiment. |

## How to read the comparisons

- **Squared error** measures how far an estimated accuracy is from the true accuracy: square that difference and average across networks. Smaller is better. “Half the squared error” does not mean half the typical error.
- **Equal compute** means equal counted computation, including the estimator’s work. It does not automatically mean equal elapsed time on a computer. The two main candidates were tested at very different budgets: about 4 million versus 3,300 complete-network-run equivalents.
- **Fresh or sealed tests** use networks whose reference answers were kept separate from the method’s development. They reduce the risk of tuning to the test answers.
- **Independently checked** means another check supports the stated result and scope. It does not mean ARC has accepted the method or that it works on all networks.
- **A mathematical guarantee** needs justified bounds covering every relevant case. An accurate estimate, or a large successful test set, is not enough by itself.

## Where to find the evidence

The detailed records below keep the original experiment identifiers, measurements, checks, limitations, and failed attempts. The two candidates are recorded as **H-E17 / T431** and **H-L1 / T317f**; the published ARC comparison and our smaller-model result as **E32 / E36 / H-U1**; rare-event estimation and repair as **H-R1 / H-R3**; probability-bound components as **H-C1**; and the latest reliability checks as **T462 / T463**. These are reference labels, not additional methods to learn.

The [owner Q&A](OWNER_QA.md#q4-13) explains why we consider the two approaches candidates and why ARC’s acceptance remains an open question.

## 1. Questions

<details>
<summary>Show detailed evidence and qualifications</summary>

| id | question | status |
|---|---|---|
| Q2 | Hilton/ARC: does per-neuron legibility add up to understanding? (AlgZoo models) | Answered for the 432-param model under one protocol. See E4 |
| Q3 | How does the 432-param AlgZoo RNN (M_16,10) compute the 2nd argmax? | Mostly answered. See [`m16/FINDINGS.md`](m16/FINDINGS.md) |
| Q4 | ARC challenge: a mechanistic estimate of M_16,10 accuracy competitive with sampling at equal compute | **Two candidates; ARC acceptance unresolved, strict deductive solution open.** Exact line-walk quadrature beats sampling on sealed clean retrains (H-E17); sampling-free-at-runtime gap walks meet our lenient comparison (H-L1). Their mechanistic status and model scope remain qualified. See [STATUS](STATUS.md) and [OWNER_QA 4.13](OWNER_QA.md#q4-13) |

**Success criteria for Q4 (re-centred on ARC's stated intent: "a scalable, algorithmic approach to producing
mechanistic estimates"; hand analysis is "a warm-up"):**
1. **Deductive**: no random model inputs. Methods that sample inputs, however cleverly (stratified,
   quadrature with model evaluations at random-shift points, control variates), are **baselines to beat**, not solutions.
2. **General**: the same code runs unchanged, with no tables or allocations fitted per model, on the example model,
   `zoo_2nd_argmax(16,10, seed=0..4)` and noisy copies; ideally also M_4,3 as a sanity check.
3. **Competitive**: MSE at most that of sampling at equal compute, with CIs, on that model set.

</details>

| E23 | Q4 crux: is the tie band-width function w compactly describable? | agent | done | [`m16/approaches/bandwidth/`](m16/approaches/bandwidth) | **No compact description; obstruction identified: cancellation.** The margin at the tie (s.d. 2.8) is a residual of large opposing terms (tie pair 39, max 34, others 19); cancellation factor 9.5-77 across seeds. So 95%-agreeing frozen gates give R^2 < 0. 5 hypotheses tested: clock/positions partial (20-24%), gates at a,b small, recency refuted (reversed), assist alone R^2 0.003, chaos qualified (smooth locally, wiggly). Best compact (3,960 params) R^2 0.39 held-out, saturates ~0.45; an MLP keeps rising (0.67 at 290k lines): learnable, not compact. delta uncorrelated across seeds on identical points (abs r < 0.03) | C (tool validated vs brute force to 3e-5) |

Low inputs ~97.5% additive in variance, but the top 5% of tie points carry 35% of the error mass (rare multi-cell bands), so Sobol barely beats iid. Linear band-edge response is off 0.95-3.7x. Flagged the leaderboard's orig truth as ~0.025 pp low: **fixed by pooling (28M samples)** | C |

On all 18 M_16,10 models: MSE ratio 0.75 [0.45,1.10] (16k), 0.60 [0.33,0.95] (64k), i.e. ~1.3-1.7x, same class as other baselines; a single fixed rule loses on seeds at 64k (1.44). Heavy-tailed face integrand from T=5 (top 1% of faces = 50-76% of the 2nd moment) | C |

Fallback 'LTQ' (deterministic quadrature, per-triple point sets) beats sampling on orig (0.11-0.28) and sigma 0.0025 (0.33-0.56) but NOT on seeds 0-4 (1.26 / 0.85) or sigma 0.005 (1.00 / 0.92). Original:: with the top-3 fixed, the best reduction (exact linear map of the gate pattern at the conditional-mean point) is off by -0.13 to -0.45 pp aggregate (2-2.5 pp per skeleton); additive (cut-HDMR) -3.5 to -3.8 pp; Gaussian with true covariance -2.1 pp. ~150 gate patterns per 256 low draws. Grid rounding shrinks the network by zeroing small W_hh weights (k=4 regions ~19k -> 29-415 at G2) but accuracy collapses (median -64 pp even at G8). Rounding W_hh alone takes fused_s0 from 87% to 40%. The units are algebraically distinct and the function depends on small recurrent weights (consistent with Hilton: the near-zero weights matter) | - |

At 1.8-2.7M FPE it wins 4, ties 3, loses 8 of 15 at equal compute (wins where pC <~ 90); the advantage grows as p falls. E2 by variant A holds (7 of 9 non-tied won at 14-21k FPE; 11/12 within 3x). E1 by variant B fails: within 2x in 14/18 but its 20-50% bias loses where sampling sees 15-160 events, and dev3_s505 is 6-10x low. **Caveat (14:18): the T218 child found a correctness bug in the continuity walk (simultaneous step-0 gate crossings when an input coordinate crosses 0 are not all toggled), affecting variant B, T349 (T340c) and T354, which run lines to large t; those numbers are suspect until the fix and rerun. T317/T317f (window t <= .5) are believed unaffected; verification pending.** **T350 walk check (14:20): its T312 walk recomputes every gate per cell, so it does not share the zero-crossing bug; a 50k-point dense forward check on dev3_s505 and seed0 agrees to grid resolution (max 9.3e-5). So T350's negative stands as a test of the method: on fresh models the E1/B part fails on its own approximations, while the E2/A part (cheap variant, 7 of 9 decided cases won) replicates.** **CORRECTION (14:23, T286 child 23310c03): the long-line walk bug is confirmed and fixed (walk3: re-gate all steps >= s* from values; at zero use the slope's sign), validated against dense forwards (max error .0005 on all lines). Corrected est/truth (variant B): E1 orig .25 / .17 / .19, seed0 .97 / 1.03 / .93, seed3 .69 / .76 / .73; E2 orig .99 / .95, seed0 1.07 / 1.22, seed3 1.40 / 3.41; variant A on E2 .96 / .94, .82 / .71, .59 / 1.23. Verdict: E1 B partial (6/9 within 3x), A fail; E2 A useful (6/6 within 2x), B partial (5/6); B's earlier E2 overshoot was mostly the bug; the target's far-gap E1 stays 4-6x low. Post hoc equal compute: B 9 / 3 / 3, A 6 / 7 / 2, better-of-two 10 / 4 / 1. T340c (K3/K5) numbers were computed with the bug and are suspended until the T218 child's rerun.** **T340 FINAL (post-fix, T286 child 948edfea, scored on the GPU truths; the T218 child's fixed B equals the T286 child's corrected B on all 15 pairs): C3 (g12 at 3 exact-law nodes, ~1.4-2.0M FPE) est/truth E1 orig 1.77 / 1.24 / .48, seed0 1.32 / 1.44 / 1.43, seed3 1.05 / 1.10 / 1.10 (8/9 within 2x); E2 1.03 / 1.01, .97 / .98, .73 / .55 (6/6 within 2x); C5 9/9 and 6/6 within 2x. Post hoc equal compute (win/tie/loss of 15): B 9/3/3, A 6/7/2, C3 4/5/6, C5 6/3/6, best per pair 11/2/2. Headline (unverified, 3 models): sampling-free estimates of error probabilities from 2e-7 to 4e-4 within 2x on 14/15 (C3) or 15/15 (C5) pairs; at ~2M FPE sampling wins wherever p >~ 1e-5, the deductive route wins where events stay rare at its own cost. Independent fresh-model test: T360.** |

Best case, an oracle giving the top-N patterns' exact error masses at c FPE each with the rest sampled (unbiased hybrid, MSE ratio q(1-q)B / (p(1-p)(B - cN)), optimised over N): c = 1 gives .995 / .981 / .94 / .86 / .73 at 1k / 4k / 16k / 64k / 256k; c = 100 gives >= .987 everywhere; bounding instead of sampling the remainder needs > 1e7 patterns at 256k. Realistic c >= 1e2-1e3 FPE (patterns found per model by LP-checked branch and bound plus a 10-d Gaussian polytope cubature each), so it ties or loses at every budget, against walk quadrature's .03-.17. Reference only: the same oracle on k = 7 prefixes would reach .18 at 256k, but prefix error masses are not exact (the closure + walk paradigm, H-S1, dead) | - |

(a) Lipschitz x Wasserstein: infinite; the weight-derived switch-point sensitivity is heavy-tailed (index 1.2-2.4) and diverges at grazing lines; even its finite floor is >= 1.7e5x the error. (b) Koksma-Hlawka / sphere-Sobolev: infinite; 3-D Vitali variation grows ~n^.6-2.3 in 50/54 slices and creases make the Hardy-Krause variation infinite; the sphere form needs smoothness s > 4, the creases cap s below 3/2. (c) Cell enclosures: the oracle width (a lower bound for any enclosure, T398 LPs included) is >= 330x at 8e13 cells and >= 900x within 10 B; 10x would need ~1e29+ cells: METHOD-limited. Independent walk (sens.py) matches the frozen per-line masses to 2e-13; ~20 CPU-min. Untried: crease-aware cells (= exact regions) and fractional mixed-smoothness classes . **Corrected by T451:** the '330x' divides by 9.5e-5; against T429's 2.0e-4 the zeroth-order oracle width is >= 157x (still > 100x), and it lower-bounds only zeroth-order enclosures; first-order crease-aware (affine) enclosures are COMPUTE-limited at the exact-region scale (~1e13-1e14 cells), not METHOD-limited at ~1e29 | T451 |

Regions do NOT favour the MLPs: distinct gate patterns in 1e7 samples D1w128 9.93M (98.6% seen once), D4w32 5.5M, M_16,10 3.2M; exact enumeration dead here too. **Walks are much easier on the shallow net:** F vs plain sampling (12-model panels, sampling-class random great circles, exact integral per circle): M_16,10 15.4 [12.5, 18.3]; D3/D4 nets 3.8-5.9; **D1w128 with the incremental exact great-circle walk (fast1.py): F .280 [.253, .306], direct check at C = 4k FPE .267** (.72 under a conservative FLOP count); F rises as depth-1 accuracy falls (D1w96 .87, D1w64 1.08). Why: on a great circle each first-layer unit flips exactly twice at closed-form angles, each flip a rank-1, 40-FLOP logit update; deeper nets and the RNN must propagate every flip through later layers. Deductive routes, all scoped negatives: exact regions; rank-order cones (comparators inexact near ties, 1.3k+ subregions per cone, not near-equivariant); a tie-band control variate (correlation <= .42). Not ARC-valid as is (random circles). Every MLP reaching .95 is at least as fragmented as M_16,10 (sampled lower bounds). Random-circle F on the 2^30 D1w128 nets .104 [.091, .115]; fixed sets there (T387 rerun): Halton mean .045, Sobol .10. Cones after the sign-check correction: the 76-77 comparators are near-exact (gate disagrees on 0.1-0.2% of unit-input pairs); the route fails on the 51-52 'both-low' units (1.3k+ subregions per cone). | T279 | Q4 Fable idea 4: CROWN-style linear-relaxation certification tree on S^9 | Claude child (T241) | done | branch claude/t241-neuron-account 69c962f6, crown_tree/README.md (pre-registered 9e5d8a2a) | **negative (unverified; its T=10 sector numbers independently match T289)**: at (16,4) zoo seed 0 (truth .99787) a sound CROWN tree leaves uncertified mass .81 / .59 / .35 at 1e4 / 3e4 / 1e5 cones (2.8e9 / 8.5e9 / 2.8e10 FLOPs), falling as ~n^-0.36; certified-wrong mass stays 0 (error regions are thinner than any leaf). The stop rule fired (23.8% of the gap >= .1 mass certified at 1e4 cones vs 90% needed); (16,5) not run. At 1e5 cones: estimate .823, bias -17.5 pp, bound +-17.7 pp; 0.5 pp uncertified would need ~1e10 cones. Cost: 1.42e5 FLOPs per cone at T=4, 99.7% back-substitution. crown_fwd is 3x cheaper and as good at T=4, but as loose as IBP at T=10. Every root generator lies on a tie face, so leaves must shrink toward every truth boundary. At T=10, full CROWN on the 180 root sectors costs 32k FPE and decides 0/180. Untried: alpha-CROWN / LP per cone, gate-space or gradient-aligned splits | - |

Shared forward bounds are no tighter than IBP. Scope: single-neuron linear relaxations (IBP, CROWN, crown_fwd) on whole sectors. | T295 | Q4 cheap regime: readout diagnostic for the mean-field closure (exact orthant; mean-field state plus exact last m steps) | Claude child (T194) | done | branch claude/t194-width16-probe b0225412, meanfield/readout/ (pre-registered bafd21fd) | **negative (unverified)**: 50 blind units on 5 DEV2 models (go: >=90% of units within 20% relative or .01 absolute). The exact Gaussian orthant on the exact sampled margin moments passes 36% (Clark 10%), so the margin law given a unit is far from Gaussian. Mean-field state through step 10-m plus exact last m steps: pass 16/18/28/30% at m = 0/1/2/3, with error falling only ~9% per exact step while branches grow 5-10x per step (N99 mean 47/698/3,518). The Gaussian state is already wrong by steps 7-9; non-Gaussianity builds up over the whole recurrence. T294's model-level corr comes from rough ordering, not calibrated probabilities. Untried: a Gaussian-mixture state through all steps; m >= 4; skew-normal readouts | - **DRI 2026-10-18: dead (verified, scoped)** by T299 (T292 child, own code, branch claude/t292-oracle-floor 26424af4, mf_verify/; pre-registered 7ce44471). The exact-moment Gaussian orthant passes 44% of units (T295 36%). An oracle Gaussian state with exact moments plus exact last m steps passes 36/38/40/48% at m = 1..4 (T295 18/28/30%; m=1 disagrees by +18 points, as expected, because T295's closure adds its own last-step error). That is about +3 points per exact step, far from the 90% go line. Scope: Gaussian-state closures with an orthant readout on the blind units, up to 4 exact last steps. Open: Gaussian-mixture states and skew readouts. |
| T294 | Q4 cheap regime: class-conditioned mean-field ReLU closure (Gaussian moment matching with switching probabilities), stage 1 on the 11 blind cases | Claude child (T194) | stage 1 done; T294b running | branch claude/t194-width16-probe 76000f4e, meanfield/README.md (pre-registered 8f067b9a) | **inconclusive by rule, first positive signal (unverified)**: per case unit (180), a joint Gaussian over (x, h) (26x26); exact linear pre-activations; exact ReLU mean and variance, Stein cross-covariances, first-order h-h terms; Clark readout; 11.9k FPE per model (unoptimised). DEV2: corr(pred, sampled) .73 (go >= .7: met; frozen gates -0.30), RMS .232 pp (go <= .15: failed), mean +.21 pp; the response is compressed ~2x (pred sd .049 vs .139). LB3 corr .80, RMS .106. Post hoc only: an affine calibration fitted on LB3 transfers to DEV2 (RMS .138), so the compression is systematic. That calibration is advice and is not counted. Next (T294b): exact bivariate ReLU second moments and the exact half-normal current input | - **T294b (pre-registered 2c1fa3a9, results 122dc026; no calibration):** on DEV2, (a) exact bivariate ReLU moments: RMS .230, corr .74, slope 1.99, 24k FPE; (b) sign-fixed current input in 16 bins: .200 / .73 / 1.83, 193k FPE; (c) both: .191 / .64 / 1.48, 392k FPE. All inconclusive, no go; stage 2 not run. Mechanism: fed the exact sampled margin mean and covariance, the Gaussian orthant still misplaces per-unit P(correct) (.789 -> .573; .689 -> .329), and Clark adds a similar error. Both pull P to the middle. So the bottleneck is the Gaussian readout (the margin law is a mixture), not the h-h closure. Next: T295, a per-unit diagnostic of exact orthant vs mean-field state plus an exact last-m-step branch mixture. |

Method: per (case, label), gates frozen at the canonical conditional-mean input give an affine logit map; model-free moments of the case and Clark's max closure give P(all margins >= 0); cost 2,759 FPE. DEV2: RMS .234 pp (go .15, stop .25) vs .265 for a fixed 8/9 rate; LB3 .120 (8/9 .123). It carries no model information: predicted sd .06 pp vs true .14, corr(pred, sampled) = -0.30. With gates frozen at one input, every model of this architecture gives about the same map; the variation comes from gate switching inside each case. Third confirmation (with T274b and T287) that frozen gates lose the model-specific signal. Untried: sub-case conditioning; a mean-field ReLU closure with switching probabilities (T294) | - |

### AlgZoo legibility (Q2)

<details>
<summary>Show detailed evidence and qualifications</summary>

| id | claim | status | evidence | alternatives / weaknesses |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| H-A1 | Legible labels do not add up to the mechanism on M_16,10 (54%) | **narrowed** (R1: driven by the 5-constant limit; one label alone drops acc to 0.79-0.95; ladder has one point) | E4 held-out scoring; E28 fixed-family sweep: 5→20 coefficients strongly improves reconstruction; M32 passes at 20, M16 does not | **result depends on the 5-constant-per-neuron rule** (free lookup tables on the same features reach 98%); one investigator, limited effort; E28 is one feature family/seed with incomplete optimization. So it shows "short labels lose skill", not "no compact description exists" |
| H-A2 | The protocol works when understanding is achievable | confirmed | E3 positive control, held-out | M_4,3 is so small that exact formulas are trivially compact |
| H-A3 | On the all-at-once network (1 hidden layer of 128 bias-free ReLU units), exact great-circle walks over a FIXED, pre-chosen set of circles estimate accuracy with MSE below sampling at ~4k FPE (F < 1) | **CONFIRMED, scoped (DRI 2026-10-19 07:2x)**: T387 (12-model panel, Halton and Sobol fixed sets all < 1) independently reproduced by T394 (different agent, own walk code, 6 fresh retrains, 10/10 fixed sets < 1, mean F .19-.37) | T387, T394 | Scope: a different architecture from ARC's M_16,10 (the owner's all-at-once question); class deterministic, ARC acceptance unknown (OWNER_QA 4.7: no random numbers, exact per circle, cross-circle average without a weight-derived bound); fixed sets are not better than random circles (T394); per-set error is shared across near-identical panel models, so the spread across sets is the honest uncertainty; one budget (~4k FPE); clean models only |

### M_16,10 mechanism (Q3)

<details>
<summary>Show detailed evidence and qualifications</summary>

| id | claim | status | evidence | alternatives / weaknesses |
|---|---|---|---|---|

The 'Fourier memory' is a description of one common configuration, not of the network |
| H-M3 | n3 and n13 are "higher harmonics" | **weak / withdrawn as a causal harmonic claim**; geometric component supported | E29 orthogonal fast-span coordinate leverage .642–.840, pair ranks 1–6 across five gates; avoids invalid nonorthogonal variance shares | Leverage is geometry, not activation variance; energy/readout-unmatched, potentially off-manifold ablations do not establish or refute causal specificity **[2026-10-18 T241 after independent review]: n3/n13 are causally used (on-manifold resampling costs 18-24 pp) but not specially important; the mode-based "most damaging" flag is withdrawn (mode damage is not specific vs random subspaces; the damped 8.37 mode is the largest). The H-M3 withdrawal of the specific harmonic-attribution claim stands.** |

| H-M7 | The network has far too many linear regions to enumerate *cheaply* | confirmed (DRI count + E14 independently: >=444k error cones, >=2.4M prefixes). Species estimate: Chao1 lower bound 2.4M (1M samples) -> 3.8M (2M samples), still rising; Good-Turing unseen mass 37-43%. Total probably 1e7-1e8+ | 535,261 distinct full gate patterns, 435,052 seen once; top 100k cover 56% of mass; 46,738 errors in 38,743 patterns | a count from a sample underestimates the true number; it does not rule out enumeration of *coarser* classes (prefixes, last-k-step gates, rank classes) |
| H-E6 | Moment/cumulant closure at the decision-margin level cannot reach ~0.1 pp | supported (E13 ceiling test) | exact cumulants per cell: -1.4 pp (2 moments), -0.35 pp (4 cumulants) | finer cells or margins conditioned on more structure might do better; single agent, not re-run ; **E35 (ARC's own Hermite-corrected cumulant propagation, 2026):** misses by 41-100 pp at every (H,T,K); root cause tested: scalar input per step makes step-1 hidden units perfectly correlated, breaking the method's small-correlation power counting (works on random RNNs with H-dim inputs, stalls with scalar inputs) |
| H-M8 | Rank order (even full ordering + number of positives) does not fix the gates precisely enough for a ~0.1 pp estimate | supported (E10) | gates frozen at the canonical point of each full ordering+K give 94.2% vs model 95.3% (-1.1 pp); restoring the 5 worst neurons' gates only raises agreement 94.6% -> 98.0% | untested: mixtures of several gate patterns per class weighted by exact polytope masses; treating the x9-comparator gates exactly |
| H-E7 | Exact path integration wastes compute because gate pieces are much finer than the correct/wrong pattern | supported (E22; consistent with E8, E16) | the indicator is correlated over 5-20 deg along circles while gate pieces are ~1.4 deg; exact-arc gain falls from 0.89 (0.2 deg) to 0.13 (full circle) | piece-skipping (exact only near argmax changes) not tried; a cheaper walker (recompute from the flipped step) gives at most 0.23-0.64x |
| H-E8 | Additive (generating-function) models of the tie band cannot reach the needed precision | supported (E26 oracle test) | best-possible additive fit per configuration misses the band integral by 4.0% overall (5.5% per config); budget ~3% | only one tie family tested in depth; interaction terms of order 2 not tried as an oracle |
| H-E9 | **Root cause of all deductive failures: catastrophic cancellation in the tie margin** | **refuted as root cause (E25 H x T scan)**: cancellation factor does not track the collapse (1732 at a collapsed H=32,T=10; 100 at a still-winning H=8,T=4). Cancellation is real (E23) but not the obstruction. Earlier note: cancellation is LARGEST at short T (median factor 2550 at T=3 vs 110 at T=10) where deductive quadrature is near exact, so cancellation alone is not the obstruction; the better-supported obstruction is irregularity / heavy tails of the band integrand growing with sequence length (collapse at T=4->5). Original evidence: E23, 5 seeds + noisy copies | margin s.d. 2.8 vs component s.d. 39/34/19; cancellation factor 9.5-77; approximations with 95% gate agreement give R^2 < 0 | not tested in hidden-state/eigenmode bases; 1-2 tie surface not studied; a method that tracks the residual exactly (not its components) is not ruled out |
| H-E10 | Deterministic summaries of the low inputs (mean field, canonical points, most likely gates) systematically OVERestimate accuracy | supported (E27: 60/60 positive; consistent with probe v2-v4, observer, FORM/SORM all biased upward) | Jensen-type effect: low inputs act as noise on a cancellation-residual margin (H-E9); removing the noise removes errors | a stochastic reduction that carries the low inputs as noise was not tried ; **qualifier (E31):** with the top 3 fixed, low inputs at their conditional mean give -0.03 +- 0.08 pp on orig (no upward bias), but +0.18/+0.84 pp on the DEV noisy copies and +0.2..+0.5 pp with top 4-5 fixed; per-skeleton errors are 3-4 pp in both directions, so the size of the Jensen effect depends on the summary |
| H-E11 | Deterministic tie-slicing (fixed-Sobol face quadrature, class *quadrature*) beats sampling by 10^2-10^3x at T=3-4 and declines gradually (~15-20x per T step to T=5, then ~2x per step); the decline tracks the band integrand's tail share | **confirmed (revised) by DRI 2026-10-17**: E25 (one seed) independently replicated by E33 (5 seeds, 32 rule seeds, iid control, different script/agent) | H=16: T5 9.4x (4.7-15), T6 3.4x, T10 1.6x; collapse below 10x at T=6 (T=5 for one seed); H=32 shifts it by ~1.6x in gain, not a full T step; within-T tail rho -0.55 | applies to well-trained models only (undertrained H=8 fails in the bulk); threshold depends on budget (E33 budget check); a quadrature result, not mechanistic in ARC's sense (L10) **Mechanism update 2026-10-18 (T272, T277; unverified):** neither kink density (saturates by T=5, wrong-sign correlation) nor a dimension factor (alpha ~0.5-0.67 at every T, rho .03) explains the decline; the gain falls through the prefactor (integrand variance relative to p(1-p)) at an unchanged rate. **Scope correction (T397, 10-19 06:4x): the T10 figure (1.6x) used claimed, not executed, cost (executed is 2.3-2.5x higher) on the 5 zoo seeds; on 100 M_16,10 retrains (DEV3) E33 loses (2.28 at 16k, 1.45 at 64k).** |
| H-M6 | Group roles (clocks, comparators, ...) via mean-ablation | supported for 13/16 neurons in groups (n2, n5, n9 not ablated); "all 16 causally confirmed" was an overclaim | ablation table | mean-ablation can break the network off-distribution, so damage does not prove the role; no resample-ablation or patching control |

Plain sampling at equal compute still has ~2.6x lower MSE; strict fails.** | **measured on two sealed panels (DRI 2026-10-18)**: independent scorer; estimates reproduced by a second implementation (T312/T320, the same agent for both checks: a second, different agent is pending, T324); cost recount within ~9% | Scope: retrains of the zoo config only; the 10x bar is a project convention, not ARC's ('match'); ~30 DEV2 variants and 5 HP2 submissions in the lineage (HP3 scored the frozen T317c once, then T317d); T317c sets two model-free constants by seeded MC at import (T317d uses exact quadrature: HP3 5.07); a panel-mean constant scores F ~13-14 under the 1k floor, so the positive is information content (n* ~1.1k FPE vs ~73 for the prior) | Strict bar; noise robustness; the per-approximation error budget (T324); est(M_16,10 target) vs truth (T281) |

H-R1 holds for p >~ 2e-7 only.** **Extended (T366, 16:59, sealed fresh test): on 4 fresh DEV3 models never used for rare events, at model-free thresholds (true p 2.7e-9 to 7.2e-7), the K-node walk estimates are within 2x on 8/8 (fix: 1.10-1.30; plain K3 also 8/8, .88-1.39, at 0.36-0.47M FPE, where plain MC at that cost expects .005-1.1 events). Across T364 + T366 fresh models, K-node walks are within 1.3x on 10/10 pairs from 1.8e-10 to 7e-7. They FAIL on triple-tie-dominated models (the target M_16,10 at p <~ 4e-8, and s527), where only the graded-g12 fix helps (dev 5/6 vs 2/6) and the target's deepest point (.24) is limited by the closure.** **Independent re-check of the deep-tail extension (T368, T241 child, own frozen K3, hashes asserted, d7aacfc1): 10/10 within 2x (s526 1.20 / 1.12; s528 1.39 / 1.31; s529 1.09 / .88; s530 1.23 / 1.23; s531 1.13 / 1.23), 8/10 within 1.3x, 9/10 one-signed high. So the deep-tail extension is CONFIRMED at the within-2x level (state it as 'within ~1.4x'), on non-triple-tie models only; the truths were public (checks code, not selection).** **Triple-tie models (T367, truth-blind selection, sealed truths): plain K3 can miss by up to 5 orders of magnitude (dev3_s541, 100% of hits at g12 < .1: est/truth 3.7e-5 and 2.9e-6), unpredictably; the graded-g12 fix gets s541 right (1.25, 1.35) and is within 2x on 6/8 selected pairs (K3 3/8; median |log ratio| .26 vs .74) but over-estimates when triple-tie hits are a large but partial share (s547 80%: 2.43, 2.12). So H-R1 needs the fix on triple-tie-dominated models, and the fix is not yet calibrated.** **Cause of the remaining failure (T369, 19:0x): the graded rule is converged, so the s547 overshoot is not quadrature; a closure oracle traces it to the R1W closure (lows pinned at mu(v4) and a fixed v4 position: exact lows + v4 position bring s547 from 2.19/2.48 to 1.04/1.04). No calibration was frozen; the fix stays uncalibrated on partial triple-tie models. A real fix needs a closure with spread lows and v4 position nodes (T370, est. 10-100x the cost).** **Weight-noised copies (T371, unverified, 03:2x): within 2x on 128/144 noisy points (GO); every point whose truth has < 50% triple-tie share is within 2x (110/110); misses only where the triple-tie sliver dominates. Triple-tie dominance depends on the threshold as well as the model (dev3_s528 is dominated at t0 1-1.5 but not at the deep t0 used in T366/T368), so 'non-triple-tie model' should read 'non-triple-tie (model, threshold)'.** **Graded-g12 on T371's misses (T388, unverified, 07:5x): 16/16 recovered within 2x, 143/144 noisy points within 2x, no overshoot; one partial-tie hit broken (57% tie share, .36), so the fix is still uncalibrated on partial-tie points until the blind check T403.** **Blind check (T403, independent, sealed before truths, 12:2x): on fresh tie-dominated points the graded rule is within 2x on 12/15 (80%, exactly the pre-registered line; 14/18 with the secondary set), median .95; misses: a 5x overshoot on a partial-tie point (the closure pattern, T369/T370) and a 12x undershoot where the predicted tie share was wrong (.97 vs truth .30); panel F 4.57 (lenient pass, strict fail). So on tie-dominated points the graded rule is ~80% within 2x, not reliable point by point, and its deterministic tie-share flag does not predict where it fails.** |

## 4b. State of Q4 (synthesis, 2026-10-18 14:00 UTC)

<details>
<summary>Show detailed evidence and qualifications</summary>

Full plain-language status: [`STATUS.md`](STATUS.md). Owner questions and answers: [`OWNER_QA.md`](OWNER_QA.md).
Scores use F = MSE x C_eff / mean p(1-p), C_eff = max(C, 1k FPE) (TASKS Directive 8, amendment 2). Strict bar:
F <= 1. Lenient bar: F <= 10, and a candidate also needs the upper 95% CI <= 10 (a project convention, not ARC's).
- **Not solved.** No method meets the strict bar on a sealed panel (T281). The cheap regime and its failures are
  covered in [m16/SCALING_OBSTRUCTIONS.md](m16/SCALING_OBSTRUCTIONS.md) section 9.
- **Best sampling-free estimator: T317f (T335; H-L1).** T317e plus T330's C1 frozen-gate margin screen, a declared
  approximation.
  - Fresh sealed HP4, scored once: C 3,302 FPE, **F 2.63 [1.93, 3.39]**, RMS .749 pp, bias +.436 pp, n* 1,255. This is
    a lenient pass and a strict fail.
  - Sensitivities: 2.67 [1.96, 3.45] under the screen-charge audit; 2.98 [2.19, 3.85] stacked (C 3,743). The plain-MC
    control scored .83-1.25.
  - Lineage on earlier panels: T317c HP3 5.08 [3.42, 7.04]; T317d HP3 5.07 [3.41, 7.03]; T317e is bitwise equal to
    T317d.
  - **Scope:** retrains only. The scheme was chosen among ~6 DEV2 variants.
  - **Deductive status (T324 audit):** sampling-free at runtime, but the approximation scheme was selected and
    validated by MC on DEV2, so it is not deductive in Hilton's strict sense.
- **Error budget (T324; product and tmax terms confirmed by T334, independent code).**
  - The line-set product carries ~40% of the MSE: Delta bias -.57 pp. T334: -.562 [-.623, -.502], Delta MSE -41%
    [-57%, -20%], with a per-model share of 20-57%.
  - The tmax cut carries ~20%: T324 -18%, T334 -20% [-33%, -1%].
  - Blind g12 carries -9%. All walk approximations together carry 66%, and the closure c 34%.
  - The product's overstatement (+.30-.34 pp) cancels the single anchor's understatement (-.34) at one anchor, so
    fixing either alone makes the estimate worse (T324 Addendum A).
- **Strict-bar lanes (80 DEV2; unverified unless noted).**
  - T326 (cost floor): negative, scoped to the family's knobs. The floor is ~2.2k FPE, which gives strict F ~1.9 at
    unchanged RMS.
  - T330 / T330b (rigorous screens): exact, but they save nothing (C 5,525 / 5,561 vs 5,490). Tier C became T317f.
  - T331 (tmax upward): GO = NO. Best RMS .669, at MSE x C 1.08x T317e.
  - T327 (closure re-check): reproduced T319 (dead, verified, scoped). Its arm B, the one-pattern spread lows, cuts the
    closure floor from .482 to .343 pp at no added cost.
  - T339 (spread in the estimator): NO FREEZE. Error sd .667 -> .455 and corr .978 -> .991, but bias +.758 and RMS **[T343, 14:15: does NOT replicate on fresh DEV3: sd ratio .912 [.76, 1.11] vs DEV2 .68; the variance cut was DEV2-specific]**
    .884. The walks' positive bias is the binding problem.
  - T333 (exact joint set J2): ~21k FPE extra, ~17x the ~1.2k strict allowance, so it is **out of reach**. The
    product's +.574 pp net offset lives in the L-band near both tie walls.
  - T347 (tilt correction): NO GO on the pre-registered rule (.230 vs .1). Post hoc it gives bias -.013 and RMS .667.
  - T351 (combinations): NO FREEZE; the selection keeps T317f. Spread+tilt has the best RMS (.609) but costs 6,543 FPE.
  - **T353 (cost-only rewrite of spread+tilt): GO on DEV2 (unverified).** Estimates are bitwise equal to arm A3;
    C 5,079 FPE, bias +.413, sd .451, RMS .609, F 2.60 (T317f 3.14). Frozen as `t351/estimator_h2.py`. A pre-registered
    DEV3 check is pending; if it is confirmed, this is the HP5 candidate.
  - Strict needs RMS <= ~.38 pp at ~5k FPE (T351).
  - Why the spread works is open: T338 NO GO; T341 refuted the running-max reading.
- **Noise (T337, unverified).** T317f breaks down **between .1% and .25% relative weight noise**: F 4.0 at .001,
  12.0 at .0025, 32.4 at .005. The cause is errors far from the tie faces, which the geometry assumes correct.
- **Rare events (owner priority; T340 partial positive, unverified).** Error probabilities of about 2e-7 to 4e-4 on
  orig and two seeds, against GPU truths.
  - Variant A (11-19k FPE) gets E2 within 3x in 6/6 cases but misses the E1 far-gap islands.
  - Variant B (0.21-0.32M FPE) reaches E1 est/truth .64-.89 on the seeds and .20-.29 on orig.
  - At equal compute (post hoc), each fixed variant beats sampling in 5/15 cases. The better variant per event family
    (post hoc) wins 10, ties 3 and loses 2. No single estimator wins a majority.
  - Open: T340c (T349) and the independent DEV3 check (T350).
  - **T344:** certificates are infeasible as tried (scoped negative, unverified). The only rigorous bound is
    8,200-27,500x loose.
- **Exact route: dead (H-E13; DRI ruling, scoped to region/coverage enumeration).** Counts are confirmed to k = 4.
  - T270: N6 >= 15,483,505 (exact lower bound), so the growth from k = 5 to k = 6 is at least 24.5x.
  - The T316 geometry patch shows that estT undercounts at k >= 6, and the patched run is ~35x slower.
  - The k = 10 figure (~1e13 regions) is an extrapolation.
  - Exact computation as a component (exact 1-D walks) is alive.
- **Panels.**
  - HP4 (T328) is spent on T317f.
  - HP5 (T336, seeds 400-499) is sealed with the scorer (14:08) as the reserve for one DRI-named submission.
  - DEV3 (T342, seeds 500-599, truths published) is in preparation as an open development panel. DEV2 has been used
    for ~40 variants.
- **Earlier synthesis (2026-10-17), still valid with its corrections:**
  - Deductive attempts lose by 8x to >300x (E10, E13, E14, E17, E19, E21; probe v2-v4), and LB3's mechanistic
    estimators by 37x or more.
  - srb does not beat sampling with cost charged as implemented (LB3).
  - Tie-slicing quadrature beats sampling at T = 3-4 and collapses at T ~ 5-8 (H-E11). The collapse is in the
    prefactor (T277, unverified). These wins are quadrature-class (fixed Sobol nodes) and so inductive under ARC's
    definition (T263).
  - Deterministic summaries of the low inputs overestimate accuracy (H-E10).
  - Additive descriptions of the tie band miss by 4% even as an oracle (H-E8).
- **For the comparison with ARC's reported solution (H-E5, unverified):** the decisive question is still how their
  method handles the thin, heavily fragmented near-tie error regions at T = 10 (H-E12, H-E15). Whether to contact ARC
  is the owner's decision. The DRI recommends at most a progress note (T323).

</details>

  ([m16/approaches/exact_brainstorm](m16/approaches/exact_brainstorm/README.md)); step 2 is two independent reviews.

**Open questions (Q4):**
1. **Strict bar.** Can the walk approximations' positive bias (product +.30-.34, tmax +.18, blind +.07 pp; T339) be
   removed deductively without J2's cost? The target is RMS <= ~.38 pp at ~5k FPE (T351).
   - Untried: zonotope screens (T330) and a cheaper geometry (T329).
2. **The spread closure.** Why does the alternating spread work (T341; a speculative period-2 mode)? And does its
   gain hold on DEV3 (T343)?
3. **Noise robustness.** The far-gap cut breaks first (T337). Errors far from the ties need a treatment, and this
   overlaps with the rare-event islands.
4. **Rare events.**
   - Does the family rule hold on fresh models (T350)? Does T349 remove variant B's low bias on orig?
   - Noisy copies and more models are untested (T340).
   - A bound conditional on the declared closure was not pursued (T344; it would not be rigorous).
5. **Exact methods not excluded by H-E13:** aggregation across regions (for example merging same-decision regions;
   T346), finite-state variants (T352) and shallow architectures (T345).
6. **Verification debts.**
   - H-L1 is measured but not confirmed.
   - T337, T340, T344, T347, T351 and T353 are unverified.
   - The E1/E2 rare-event results rest on one agent's code until T350 reports.
